Disparities During the COVID-19 Pandemic in SARS-CoV-2 Testing Increased After a Federal Government's At-Home Testing Distribution Program Compared with Before in a U.S. Urban Setting.

Disparities During the COVID-19 Pandemic in SARS-CoV-2 Testing Increased After a Federal Government's At-Home Testing Distribution Program Compared with Before in a U.S. Urban Setting.
作者： Penelope Schenkel, Alexandra K. Mueller, Jessica Wagner Duchen, Jamie Perin, Suzanne M. Dolwick Grieb, Kelly Lowensen, Saifuddin Ahmed, Gregory Dale Kirk, C. Dennison Himmelfarb, Jason E. Farley
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
诊断检测对于控制传染病的 传播 至关重要。这项研究解决了了解联邦家庭测试计划如何影响 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 测试差异的需要。
设计与对象
这项二次分析是使用 2021 年 2 月至 2022 年 11 月期间在美国大西洋中部城市环境中收集的随机比较有效性母体试验的基线数据进行的。该研究考察了社会人口特征、测试经验和 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 测试采用率之间的关联。
核心方法
使用具有稳健标准误差的逻辑回归模型来分析数据。该研究的重点是评估联邦家庭测试计划与测试使用率之间的关联,以及检查与社会人口因素的相互作用。
主要结果
Overall, 79.9% (1486) of participants reported receiving a COVID-19 test. Exposure to the federal testing program, higher education, private health insurance, higher household income, and ease of testing access were significantly associated with testing. After adjustment, private insurance (adjusted OR [aOR] = 1.61; 95% CI: 1.12, 2.31) and higher income (aOR = 1.50; 95% CI: 1.12, 2.02) remained associated with testing.
解释与创新
调查结果表明,与实施前相比,在联邦测试计划期间,按社会经济地位划分的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 测试的差异有所增加。这凸显了采取有针对性的干预措施的必要性,以确保公平地获得诊断检测。
科研与公卫意义
结果强调了公平获得诊断检测对于有效公共卫生应对措施的重要性。确保所有人群都有平等的机会接受检测有助于减少差距并改善总体流行病控制。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于二次分析的基线数据,这可能会限制见解的深度。证据是中等的,因为它依赖于观察数据和调整后的模型,但研究结果是以报告的关联和统计分析为基础的。

Disparities During the COVID-19 Pandemic in SARS-CoV-2 Testing Increased After a Federal Government's At-Home Testing Distribution Program Compared with Before in a U.S. Urban Setting.

Authors: Penelope Schenkel, Alexandra K. Mueller, Jessica Wagner Duchen, Jamie Perin, Suzanne M. Dolwick Grieb, Kelly Lowensen, Saifuddin Ahmed, Gregory Dale Kirk, C. Dennison Himmelfarb, Jason E. Farley
Original abstract (English)
Diagnostic testing is essential for controlling the transmission of infectious diseases. During the COVID-19 pandemic, testing became widely available in the United States. However, the association between a federal at-home COVID-19 rapid antigen test distribution program and sociodemographics and COVID-19 testing remains unknown. This secondary analysis aims to (1) assess the association between the federal at-home testing program and testing uptake and (2) examine associations, including interactions with the testing program, between sociodemographic characteristics, testing experience, and COVID-19 testing. We analyzed baseline data collected from February 2021 to November 2022 in a randomized comparative effectiveness parent trial conducted in a mid-Atlantic U.S. Logistic regression models with robust standard errors were used. Overall, 79.9% (1486) of participants reported receiving a COVID-19 test. Exposure to the federal testing program (odds ratio [OR] = 2.25; 95% confidence interval [CI]: 1.61, 3.13), higher education (OR = 1.36; 95% CI: 1.02, 1.80), private health insurance (OR = 2.03; 95% CI: 1.41, 2.92), higher household income (OR = 1.49; 95% CI: 1.06, 2.09), and ease of testing access (OR = 1.40; 95% CI: 1.04, 1.87) were significantly associated with testing. After adjustment, private insurance (adjusted OR [aOR] = 1.61; 95% CI: 1.12, 2.31) and higher income (aOR = 1.50; 95% CI: 1.12, 2.02) remained associated with testing. The adjusted odds of COVID-19 testing were 2-fold higher (aOR = 2.21; 95% CI: 1.21, 4.04) for high-income (vs. low-income) households during the federal at-home COVID-19 rapid antigen testing program than before its implementation. Disparities in COVID-19 testing by socioeconomic status increased during the federal testing program compared with the period before its implementation. Ensuring equitable access to diagnostic testing remains critical for an effective public health response.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Diagnostic testing is essential for controlling the transmission of infectious diseases. This study addresses the need to understand how a federal at-home testing program affects disparities in SARS-CoV-2 testing.
Design and population
This secondary analysis was conducted using baseline data from a randomized comparative effectiveness parent trial in a mid-Atlantic U.S. urban setting, collected between February 2021 and November 2022. The study examined associations between sociodemographic characteristics, testing experience, and SARS-CoV-2 testing uptake.
Core methods
Logistic regression models with robust standard errors were used to analyze the data. The study focused on assessing the association between the federal at-home testing program and testing uptake, as well as examining interactions with sociodemographic factors.
Main results
Overall, 79.9% (1486) of participants reported receiving a COVID-19 test. Exposure to the federal testing program, higher education, private health insurance, higher household income, and ease of testing access were significantly associated with testing. After adjustment, private insurance (adjusted OR [aOR] = 1.61; 95% CI: 1.12, 2.31) and higher income (aOR = 1.50; 95% CI: 1.12, 2.02) remained associated with testing.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings indicate that disparities in SARS-CoV-2 testing by socioeconomic status increased during the federal testing program compared with the period before its implementation. This highlights the need for targeted interventions to ensure equitable access to diagnostic testing.
Scientific and public-health significance
The results emphasize the importance of equitable access to diagnostic testing for an effective public health response. Ensuring that all populations have equal opportunities to access testing can help reduce disparities and improve overall pandemic control.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on baseline data from a secondary analysis, which may limit the depth of insights. The evidence is moderate, as it relies on observational data and adjusted models, but the findings are grounded in the reported associations and statistical analyses.

Nebulized interferon-β1a (SNG001) in COVID-19 patients: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials.

Nebulized interferon-β1a (SNG001) in COVID-19 patients: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials.
作者： Jin-Qiao Qin, Gui-Zuo Wang, Dong Hee Han
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
This systematic review and meta-analysis aimed to determine the efficacy of nebulised interferon-β1a (SNG001) in patients with Coronavirus disease-2019 (COVID-19).

研究检索截至 2025 年 11 月。
共识与结论
Nebulised SNG001 was safe and associated with a reduction in COVID-19-related hospital admission.
争议与缺口
所提供的证据没有明确指出争议或证据差距。
科研与实践启示

Nebulized interferon-β1a (SNG001) in COVID-19 patients: A systematic review and meta-analysis of randomized controlled trials.

Authors: Jin-Qiao Qin, Gui-Zuo Wang, Dong Hee Han
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: This systematic review and meta-analysis aimed to determine the efficacy of nebulised interferon-β1a (SNG001) in patients with Coronavirus disease-2019 (COVID-19). METHODS: A systematic search was conducted across PubMed, Embase, Cochrane Library, and clinicaltrials.gov, without language restrictions. Studies were searched up to November 2025. Randomised controlled trials (RCTs) on treatment of COVID-19 with SNG001, compared with placebo or standard of care, were reviewed. Data were analysed using the RevMan Version 5.1 (The Cochrane Collaboration). Pooled risk ratios (RRs) with 95% confidence intervals (CIs) were calculated for dichotomous outcomes. RESULTS: Four RCTs (enrolling 1055 participants) met the inclusion criteria. The proportion of patients with hospitalisations (data extracted from two RCTs) (RR 0.16, 95% CI 0.03 to 0.88), and serious adverse events (RR 0.65, 95% CI 0.47 to 0.90) were significantly fewer in SNG001 groups. There was no statistically significant difference in all-cause mortality (RR 0.72, 95% CI 0.37 to 1.39), patients non-recovery at day 28 (RR 0.87, 95% CI 0.65 to 1.17), and adverse event (RR 0.97, 95% CI 0.90 to 1.04) between the two groups. CONCLUSIONS: Nebulised SNG001 was safe and associated with a reduction in COVID-19-related hospital admission. SNG001 shows a potential benefit for outpatients with COVID-19, but it requires larger clinical trials for future validations, particularly in vaccinated and previously infected populations.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This systematic review and meta-analysis aimed to determine the efficacy of nebulised interferon-β1a (SNG001) in patients with Coronavirus disease-2019 (COVID-19).
Evidence base and method
Studies were searched up to November 2025.
Consensus and conclusions
Nebulised SNG001 was safe and associated with a reduction in COVID-19-related hospital admission.
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not clearly state controversies or evidence gaps.
Research and practice implications
SNG001 shows a potential benefit for outpatients with COVID-19, but it requires larger clinical trials for future validations, particularly in vaccinated and previously infected populations.

[Dynamics of behavioral factors and attitudes toward vaccination in moscow during the COVID-19 pandemic: from the acute phase to adaptation].

[Dynamics of behavioral factors and attitudes toward vaccination in moscow during the COVID-19 pandemic: from the acute phase to adaptation].
作者： I V Bogdan, D P Chistyakova, E I Aksenova, N N Kamynina, T I Bonkalo, A V Pravednikov, A A Dreneva, V N Stepanova, E V Dmitrieva
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review analyzes the dynamics of awareness, preventive behavior, trust, emotional state, and attitudes toward vaccination among Moscow residents during the transition from the acute phase to adaptation of the COVID-19 pandemic in 2021 compared with 2020.
证据基础与方法
该调查工具是由世卫组织欧洲区域办事处在爱尔福特大学的支持下开发的,并根据莫斯科的情况进行了调整。CATI 是使用分层双框架样本进行的。文章分析了第 4 次和第 5 次调查波:2021 年 2 月(n = 1038)和 2021 年 7 月至 8 月(n = 998)。
共识与结论
到第5次调查波次时,大多数预防措施的依从性下降,尤其是社交距离、限制接触和使用洗手液。在取消佩戴手套要求后,不戴手套的比例急剧上升。情绪状态的自我评估均值下降至3.47分;机构评估下降,对疫苗接种的态度变得更加两极分化。
争议与缺口
审查强调了对预防措施的遵守率下降以及 疫苗接种 态度的两极分化。然而,所使用的方法可能有局限性,并且数据并不能完全说明这些变化背后的原因。
科研与实践启示
公共卫生战略应结合一致的科学沟通、与犹豫不决的群体开展有针对性的工作以及横向信任。

[Dynamics of behavioral factors and attitudes toward vaccination in moscow during the COVID-19 pandemic: from the acute phase to adaptation].

Authors: I V Bogdan, D P Chistyakova, E I Aksenova, N N Kamynina, T I Bonkalo, A V Pravednikov, A A Dreneva, V N Stepanova, E V Dmitrieva
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: The transition of the COVID-19 pandemic from the acute phase to adaptation was accompanied by changes in preventive behavior, institutional trust, emotional state and attitudes toward vaccination. OBJECTIVE: To analyze the dynamics of awareness, preventive behavior, trust, emotional state and attitudes toward vaccination among Moscow residents in 2021 compared with 2020. MATERIALS AND METHODS: The survey tool was developed by the WHO Regional Office for Europe with support from the University of Erfurt and adapted to the Moscow context. CATI was conducted using a stratified dual-frame sample. The article analyzes the 4th and 5th survey waves: February 2021 (n = 1038) and July-August 2021 (n = 998). RESULTS: By the 5th wave, adherence to most preventive measures decreased, especially social distancing, limiting contacts and using hand sanitizers. The proportion not wearing gloves increased sharply after the requirement was lifted. The mean self-assessment of emotional state decreased to 3.47 points; institutional assessments declined and vaccination attitudes became more polarized. CONCLUSIONS: Public health strategies should combine consistent scientific communication, targeted work with vaccine-hesitant groups and horizontal trust.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review analyzes the dynamics of awareness, preventive behavior, trust, emotional state, and attitudes toward vaccination among Moscow residents during the transition from the acute phase to adaptation of the COVID-19 pandemic in 2021 compared with 2020.
Evidence base and method
The survey tool was developed by the WHO Regional Office for Europe with support from the University of Erfurt and adapted to the Moscow context. CATI was conducted using a stratified dual-frame sample. The article analyzes the 4th and 5th survey waves: February 2021 (n = 1038) and July-August 2021 (n = 998).
Consensus and conclusions
By the 5th wave, adherence to most preventive measures decreased, especially social distancing, limiting contacts, and using hand sanitizers. The proportion not wearing gloves increased sharply after the requirement was lifted. The mean self-assessment of emotional state decreased to 3.47 points; institutional assessments declined and vaccination attitudes became more polarized.
Controversies and gaps
The review highlights the decrease in adherence to preventive measures and the polarization of vaccination attitudes. However, the methods used may have limitations, and the data do not fully address the reasons behind these changes.
Research and practice implications
Public health strategies should combine consistent scientific communication, targeted work with vaccine-hesitant groups, and horizontal trust.

感染回声:长新冠 中的病毒持久性、免疫激活和组织病理学变化:系统评价

Echoes of infection: viral persistence, immune activation, and histopathological changes in long COVID: a systematic review
作者： Abdelrahman Elmahroky, Yahya Aly, Aser Farghaly, Hisham Abdelrasoul, Haneen Suliman, Menna Badawi, Omar Walid Tanbedawy, Abdelrahman Emad Helmy, Omnia Azmy Nabeh
摘要中文翻译长新冠 或 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 (PASC) 的急性后遗症影响 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染后估计 10-20% 的个体。尽管其给全球带来了巨大的负担,但其病理生理机制仍不完全清楚。本系统综述旨在综合关于跨多个器官系统的病毒持久性、免疫激活和组织病理学变化的现有证据,借鉴对 长新冠 个体的研究以及更广泛的急性后 队列研究、尸检调查和与 长新冠 病理生理学相关的基于病例的报告。遵循 PRISMA 指南并持有 PROSPERO 注册 (CRD420261403869),在 PubMed、Scopus、EMBASE 和 Cochrane CENTRAL 上进行了系统文献检索。合格的报告被分层为(A)在12周或之后评估的症状定义的长新冠,(B)没有一致确认的长新冠定义或时间的急性后或混合人群,(C)尸检研究,以及(D)孤立的病例/胎儿报告。研究结果按样本、分析目标和分析单位进行叙述性综合。JBI 检查表用于项目级评估,而不是证据确定性评级。其中包括 45 份报告:A 层 16 份、B 层 23 份、三份尸检研究和三份孤立病例/胎儿报告。在选定的组织和血液隔室中报告了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 基因组 RNA、病毒蛋白和与近期病毒活性兼容的标记物,但检测结果因群体选择、时间、标本和测定而异。没有纳入的研究通过恢复感染性病毒来确定复制能力。组织病理学异常包括炎症浸润、纤维化和血管损伤;然而,长新冠 的因果归因受到急性疾病严重程度、活检指征、合并症和不完整的比较数据的限制。由于 队列研究 重叠、重复抽样和特定结果分母,未报告单个参与者的总数。该综述支持 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染后的生物异质性,包括残留病毒物质、免疫失调、血管损伤和适应不良组织修复。器官特异性 患病率、组织向性层次、复制能力 储存宿主 以及 抗病毒药物 或免疫调节 治疗 的优越性仍然未定义。需要标准化的 长新冠 定义、代表性的 队列研究、经过验证的测定、适当的比较器以及将生物标志物与临床结果联系起来的纵向研究。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
长新冠 或 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 (PASC) 的急性后遗症影响 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染后估计 10-20% 的个体。本系统综述旨在综合关于跨多个器官系统的病毒持久性、免疫激活和组织病理学变化的现有证据,借鉴对 长新冠 个体的研究以及更广泛的急性后 队列研究、尸检调查和与 长新冠 病理生理学相关的基于病例的报告。
证据基础与方法
遵循 PRISMA 指南并持有 PROSPERO 注册 (CRD420261403869),在 PubMed、Scopus、EMBASE 和 Cochrane CENTRAL 上进行了系统文献检索。合格的报告被分层为(A)在12周或之后评估的症状定义的长新冠,(B)没有一致确认的长新冠定义或时间的急性后或混合人群,(C)尸检研究,以及(D)孤立的病例/胎儿报告。研究结果按样本、分析目标和分析单位进行叙述性综合。JBI 检查表用于项目级评估,而不是证据确定性评级。
共识与结论
该综述支持 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染后的生物异质性,包括残留病毒物质、免疫失调、血管损伤和适应不良组织修复。在选定的组织和血液隔室中报告了 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 基因组 RNA、病毒蛋白和与近期病毒活性兼容的标记物,但检测结果因群体选择、时间、标本和测定而异。
争议与缺口
没有纳入的研究通过恢复感染性病毒来确定复制能力。组织病理学异常包括炎症浸润、纤维化和血管损伤;然而,长新冠 的因果归因受到急性疾病严重程度、活检指征、合并症和不完整的比较数据的限制。由于 队列研究 重叠、重复抽样和特定结果分母,未报告单个参与者的总数。
科研与实践启示
器官特异性 患病率、组织向性层次、复制能力 储存宿主 以及 抗病毒药物 或免疫调节 治疗 的优越性仍然未定义。需要标准化的 长新冠 定义、代表性的 队列研究、经过验证的测定、适当的比较器以及将生物标志物与临床结果联系起来的纵向研究。

Echoes of infection: viral persistence, immune activation, and histopathological changes in long COVID: a systematic review

Authors: Abdelrahman Elmahroky, Yahya Aly, Aser Farghaly, Hisham Abdelrasoul, Haneen Suliman, Menna Badawi, Omar Walid Tanbedawy, Abdelrahman Emad Helmy, Omnia Azmy Nabeh
Original abstract (English)
Long COVID, or post-acute sequelae of SARS-CoV-2 (PASC), affects an estimated 10–20% of individuals following SARS-CoV-2 infection. Despite its substantial global burden, the pathophysiological mechanisms remain incompletely understood. This systematic review aimed to synthesize available evidence on viral persistence, immune activation, and histopathological changes across multiple organ systems, drawing from studies of individuals with Long COVID as well as broader post-acute cohorts, autopsy investigations, and case-based reports relevant to Long COVID pathophysiology. Following PRISMA guidelines and holding a PROSPERO registration (CRD420261403869), a systematic literature search was conducted across PubMed, Scopus, EMBASE, and Cochrane CENTRAL. Eligible reports were stratified as (A) symptom-defined Long COVID assessed at or after 12 weeks, (B) post-acute or mixed populations without a consistently confirmed Long COVID definition or timing, (C) autopsy studies, and (D) isolated case/fetal reports. Findings were synthesized narratively by specimen, assay target, and unit of analysis. JBI checklists were used for item-level appraisal, not as certainty-of-evidence ratings. Forty-five reports were included: 16 in stratum A, 23 in stratum B, three autopsy studies, and three isolated case/fetal reports. SARS-CoV-2 genomic RNA, viral proteins, and markers compatible with recent viral activity were reported in selected tissue and blood compartments, but detection varied with population selection, timing, specimen, and assay. No included study established replication competence by recovery of infectious virus. Histopathological abnormalities included inflammatory infiltrates, fibrosis, and vascular injury; however, causal attribution to Long COVID was limited by acute disease severity, biopsy indication, comorbidity, and incomplete comparator data. A single participant total was not reported because of overlapping cohorts, repeated sampling, and outcome-specific denominators. The review supports biological heterogeneity after SARS-CoV-2 infection, including residual viral material, immune dysregulation, vascular injury, and maladaptive tissue repair. Organ-specific prevalence, a hierarchy of tissue tropism, replication-competent reservoirs, and superiority of antiviral or immunomodulatory treatment remain undefined. Standardized Long COVID definitions, representative cohorts, validated assays, appropriate comparators, and longitudinal studies linking biological markers to clinical outcomes are required.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Long COVID, or post-acute sequelae of SARS-CoV-2 (PASC), affects an estimated 10–20% of individuals following SARS-CoV-2 infection. This systematic review aimed to synthesize available evidence on viral persistence, immune activation, and histopathological changes across multiple organ systems, drawing from studies of individuals with Long COVID as well as broader post-acute cohorts, autopsy investigations, and case-based reports relevant to Long COVID pathophysiology.
Evidence base and method
Following PRISMA guidelines and holding a PROSPERO registration (CRD420261403869), a systematic literature search was conducted across PubMed, Scopus, EMBASE, and Cochrane CENTRAL. Eligible reports were stratified as (A) symptom-defined Long COVID assessed at or after 12 weeks, (B) post-acute or mixed populations without a consistently confirmed Long COVID definition or timing, (C) autopsy studies, and (D) isolated case/fetal reports. Findings were synthesized narratively by specimen, assay target, and unit of analysis. JBI checklists were used for item-level appraisal, not as certainty-of-evidence ratings.
Consensus and conclusions
The review supports biological heterogeneity after SARS-CoV-2 infection, including residual viral material, immune dysregulation, vascular injury, and maladaptive tissue repair. SARS-CoV-2 genomic RNA, viral proteins, and markers compatible with recent viral activity were reported in selected tissue and blood compartments, but detection varied with population selection, timing, specimen, and assay.
Controversies and gaps
No included study established replication competence by recovery of infectious virus. Histopathological abnormalities included inflammatory infiltrates, fibrosis, and vascular injury; however, causal attribution to Long COVID was limited by acute disease severity, biopsy indication, comorbidity, and incomplete comparator data. A single participant total was not reported because of overlapping cohorts, repeated sampling, and outcome-specific denominators.
Research and practice implications
Organ-specific prevalence, a hierarchy of tissue tropism, replication-competent reservoirs, and superiority of antiviral or immunomodulatory treatment remain undefined. Standardized Long COVID definitions, representative cohorts, validated assays, appropriate comparators, and longitudinal studies linking biological markers to clinical outcomes are required.

A dose of reality: autodisable syringe supply challenges in the COVID-19 immunization response.

A dose of reality: autodisable syringe supply challenges in the COVID-19 immunization response.
作者： Tara M. Herrick, Liliana Cavazos-Luppino, Elizabeth Griffin, Paul Mallins, Robert Matthews, Murat Hakan Ozturk, Ryan Pinza, Surabhi Rajaram, Courtney Jarrahian
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
自动禁用 (AD) 注射器对于安全免疫至关重要,特别是在低收入和中等收入国家,因为它们可以防止重复使用并降低疾病风险 传播。
证据基础与方法
Prior to the COVID-19 pandemic, global supply was stable and met routine immunization needs. The rapid rollout of COVID-19 vaccines led to a surge in demand for AD syringes, including those with nonstandard dose volumes, which required new product development and matching of syringes to specific vaccines-a synchronization that is typically not needed.
共识与结论
然而,这场大流行凸显了协调 疫苗 和注射设备供应链的协调挑战。尽管生产能力扩大,但注射器短缺仍然出现,增加了剂量错误的风险,给卫生系统带来了额外的压力。
争议与缺口
Limited early information on COVID-19 vaccine dose volume specifications, long lead times, and lack of coordination between vaccine and syringe stakeholders created forecasting difficulties.
科研与实践启示
As demand later declined, oversupply and market instability followed, prompting contract adjustments. This paper incorporates lessons learned from the COVID-19 response and emphasizes that syringes must not be an afterthought in immunization planning for both future outbreak preparedness and routine immunization efforts.

A dose of reality: autodisable syringe supply challenges in the COVID-19 immunization response.

Authors: Tara M. Herrick, Liliana Cavazos-Luppino, Elizabeth Griffin, Paul Mallins, Robert Matthews, Murat Hakan Ozturk, Ryan Pinza, Surabhi Rajaram, Courtney Jarrahian
Original abstract (English)
Autodisable (AD) syringes are vital for safe immunization, particularly in low- and middle-income countries, as they prevent reuse and reduce the risk of disease transmission. Prior to the COVID-19 pandemic, global supply was stable and met routine immunization needs. The pandemic, however, highlighted coordination challenges in aligning vaccine and injection device supply chains. The rapid rollout of COVID-19 vaccines led to a surge in demand for AD syringes, including those with nonstandard dose volumes, which required new product development and matching of syringes to specific vaccines-a synchronization that is typically not needed. Limited early information on COVID-19 vaccine dose volume specifications, long lead times, and lack of coordination between vaccine and syringe stakeholders created forecasting difficulties. Procurement agencies faced increased costs and logistical constraints and had to quickly adapt contracts and supply strategies. Although manufacturing capacity expanded, syringe shortages still emerged, increasing the risk of dosing errors and placing additional strain on health systems. As demand later declined, oversupply and market instability followed, prompting contract adjustments. This paper incorporates lessons learned from the COVID-19 response and emphasizes that syringes must not be an afterthought in immunization planning for both future outbreak preparedness and routine immunization efforts. It also outlines key strategies for strengthening syringe market resilience.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Autodisable (AD) syringes are vital for safe immunization, particularly in low- and middle-income countries, as they prevent reuse and reduce the risk of disease transmission.
Evidence base and method
Prior to the COVID-19 pandemic, global supply was stable and met routine immunization needs. The rapid rollout of COVID-19 vaccines led to a surge in demand for AD syringes, including those with nonstandard dose volumes, which required new product development and matching of syringes to specific vaccines-a synchronization that is typically not needed.
Consensus and conclusions
The pandemic, however, highlighted coordination challenges in aligning vaccine and injection device supply chains. Although manufacturing capacity expanded, syringe shortages still emerged, increasing the risk of dosing errors and placing additional strain on health systems.
Controversies and gaps
Limited early information on COVID-19 vaccine dose volume specifications, long lead times, and lack of coordination between vaccine and syringe stakeholders created forecasting difficulties.
Research and practice implications
As demand later declined, oversupply and market instability followed, prompting contract adjustments. This paper incorporates lessons learned from the COVID-19 response and emphasizes that syringes must not be an afterthought in immunization planning for both future outbreak preparedness and routine immunization efforts.

严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Mpro 抑制剂的时间依赖性活性。

Time-Dependent Activity of SARS-CoV-2 Mpro Inhibitors.
作者： Jinnette Tolentino Collado, Ming-Qian Wang, S. X. Li, Matthew T. Cifone, Alyssa C. Pollard, D. Kozakov, J. Hearing, P. Tonge
摘要中文翻译新型冠状病毒感染 大流行导致了 疫苗 和 抗病毒药物 药物的快速开发,以预防严重疾病和死亡。抑制重要的主要病毒蛋白酶 Mpro 的几种药物已投入临床使用(Voget R,Gutschow M. 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Main Protease Inhibitors Early Kinetic Characterization of 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Main Protease Inhibitors: A Review and Guidance for Biochemical Assessments. 2026;65:860-81.),并且正在开发其他 Mpro 抑制剂以解决耐药性和新型冠状病毒的问题。早期药物发现和开发主要使用非细胞生化测定来表征候选药物的药代动力学特性(Tonge PJ。药物发现中的药物靶标动力学。由于药物消除后仍与靶标结合的药物可能具有延长的活性,从而使剂量频率降低,因此我们评估了四种具有不同作用机制的 Mpro 抑制剂的靶标停留时间与 抗病毒药物 活性之间的关系。这包括不可逆抑制剂 N3、非共价抑制剂 ensitrelvir、其滞留时间为 2.4 分钟,可逆共价抑制剂 13b 和 nirmatrelvir 的滞留时间分别为 19 和 23.3 分钟。为了将体外滞留时间与时间依赖性 抗病毒药物 活性相关联,我们用 抗病毒药物 感染的 Vero-E6 细胞进行了洗脱实验,虽然所有化合物均抑制病毒 RNA 产生,但滞留时间最短的是 ensitrelvir。细胞内清除实验证实,ensitrelvir 在 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染的细胞中的停留时间明显低于其他化合物,低于新生病毒复制酶/转录酶复合物的功能寿命。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该研究重点关注严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2,评估四种Mpro抑制剂的目标停留时间与其抗病毒药物活性之间的关系。核心问题是药物靶标结合的持续时间是否会影响这些抑制剂对 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的有效性。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对 Mpro 抑制剂的药代动力学特性的系统分析。它包括用感染 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 的 Vero-E6 细胞进行体外冲洗实验,以将停留时间与 抗病毒药物 活性相关联。证据来自生化测定和药物-靶标相互作用的实验数据。
共识与结论
研究得出结论,Mpro 抑制剂在 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染细胞中的停留时间与其 抗病毒药物 活性相关。具体而言,与滞留时间最短且清洗后活性较低的恩斯特韦相比,滞留时间较长的化合物(例如 nirmatrelvir)显示出更高的 抗病毒药物 功效。
争议与缺口
这些证据凸显了早期药物发现方法的潜在局限性,这些方法主要使用非细胞分析。这可能导致对体内药物行为的不完全理解。此外,该研究并未解决这些抑制剂的长期影响或其​​针对新兴 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体的性能。
科研与实践启示
研究结果表明,未来的药物开发应优先考虑目标停留时间较长的化合物,以增强 抗病毒药物 的疗效。还需要更全面的体外和体内研究来评估 Mpro 抑制剂对新 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 变体的有效性并完善给药策略。

Time-Dependent Activity of SARS-CoV-2 Mpro Inhibitors.

Authors: Jinnette Tolentino Collado, Ming-Qian Wang, S. X. Li, Matthew T. Cifone, Alyssa C. Pollard, D. Kozakov, J. Hearing, P. Tonge
Original abstract (English)
The COVID-19 pandemic led to the rapid development of vaccines and antiviral drugs to prevent severe disease and death. Several drugs that inhibit the essential main viral protease Mpro are in clinical use (Voget R, Gutschow M. Early Kinetic Characterization of SARS-CoV-2 Main Protease Inhibitors: A Review and Guidance for Biochemical Assessments. 2026;65:860-81.) and additional Mpro inhibitors are being developed to address drug-resistance and novel emergent coronaviruses. Early drug discovery and development has primarily used non-cell-based biochemical assays to characterize the pharmacokinetic properties of drug candidates (Tonge PJ. Drug-target kinetics in drug discovery. Because drugs that remain bound to the target may have prolonged activity following drug elimination enabling dose frequency to be reduced, we evaluated the relationship between target residence time and antiviral activity of four Mpro inhibitors with different mechanisms of action. This included the irreversible inhibitor N3, the noncovalent inhibitor ensitrelvir, which had a residence time of 2.4 min, and the reversible covalent inhibitors 13b and nirmatrelvir, which had residence times of 19 and 23.3 min, respectively. To correlate the in vitro residence times with time-dependent antiviral activity, we performed washout experiments with Vero-E6 cells infected with SARS-CoV-2. While all the compounds inhibited viral RNA production, ensitrelvir with the shortest residence time was significantly less active than the other compounds following washout. The intracellular washout experiments confirmed that ensitrelvir exhibited a shorter residence time in SARS-CoV-2-infected cells, falling below the functional lifespan of nascent viral replicase/transcriptase complexes.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study focuses on SARS-CoV-2 and evaluates the relationship between the target residence time of four Mpro inhibitors and their antiviral activity. The central question is whether the duration of drug-target binding influences the effectiveness of these inhibitors against SARS-CoV-2.
Evidence base and method
The review is a systematic analysis of the pharmacokinetic properties of Mpro inhibitors. It includes in vitro washout experiments with Vero-E6 cells infected with SARS-CoV-2 to correlate residence time with antiviral activity. The evidence is derived from biochemical assays and experimental data on drug-target interactions.
Consensus and conclusions
The study concludes that the residence time of Mpro inhibitors in SARS-CoV-2-infected cells is associated with their antiviral activity. Specifically, compounds with longer residence times, such as nirmatrelvir, showed greater antiviral efficacy compared to ensitrelvir, which had the shortest residence time and was less active post-washout.
Controversies and gaps
The evidence highlights a potential limitation in early drug discovery methods, which primarily used non-cell-based assays. This may have led to an incomplete understanding of in vivo drug behavior. Additionally, the study does not address the long-term effects of these inhibitors or their performance against emerging SARS-CoV-2 variants.
Research and practice implications
The findings suggest that future drug development should prioritize compounds with longer target residence times to enhance antiviral efficacy. There is also a need for more comprehensive in vitro and in vivo studies to evaluate the effectiveness of Mpro inhibitors against new SARS-CoV-2 variants and to refine dosing strategies.

Social support networks of older adults before, during and after the COVID-19 pandemic

Social support networks of older adults before, during and after the COVID-19 pandemic
作者： Marjan Cugmas, Anuška Ferligoj, Tina Kogovšek, Zenel Batagelj
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review focuses on the social support networks of older adults in Slovenia before, during, and after the COVID-19 pandemic. It examines the prevalence and characteristics of different types of social support networks, including informal support from relatives, friends, and neighbors, and how these networks have evolved over time, particularly in response to the pandemic.
证据基础与方法
该研究是 2002 年、2020 年和 2025 年在斯洛文尼亚进行的一项横断面调查,收集了老年人(65 岁以上)以自我为中心的网络数据。使用符号数据聚类和对应分析对数据进行分析。这些样本具有性别和地区的代表性。
共识与结论
该审查确定了五种不同类型的社会支持网络:家庭依赖型、私人限制型、当地自给自足型、当地一体化型和更广泛的社区网络。它还指出了一个名为“未列入名单的改变者”的群体的出现,他们报告说没有非正式的社会支持。这些网络类型的 患病率 表明,一些网络类型受疫情影响更大,而另一些网络类型则反映了长期的人口变化。到 2025 年,没有社会支持的老年人比例已下降至 4.4%,表明部分康复。
争议与缺口
人们观察到社会支持方面持续存在不平等,男性更有可能经历社会孤立。审查强调需要有针对性的社会服务来解决这些差异。However,the evidence does not provide detailed mechanisms or explanations for these inequalities,nor does it explore the impact of specific 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 variants on social support networks.
科研与实践启示
研究结果表明,社会服务应侧重于为社会孤立的老年人,特别是男性提供主动支持。Further research is needed to understand the underlying reasons for persistent inequalities and to explore how different 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 variants may influence social support dynamics.

Social support networks of older adults before, during and after the COVID-19 pandemic

Authors: Marjan Cugmas, Anuška Ferligoj, Tina Kogovšek, Zenel Batagelj
Original abstract (English)
Societies are considerably challenged by population aging, especially when it comes to providing services and support for older adults. Although formal services such as home care and volunteering address some of these needs, the informal social support given by relatives, friends and neighbours is just as vital. In Slovenia, the share of older adults reporting they have no informal social support rose from around 1% in 2002 to 7.8% in 2020, coinciding with the first year of the COVID-19 pandemic. The article examines the prevalence and characteristics of social support network types relied on by older adults in Slovenia between 2002 and 2025, with a special focus on the post-pandemic recovery. To that end, a cross-sectional survey collected ego-centred network data of older adults (65+) in Slovenia in 2002, 2020 and 2025. The samples are representative of both gender and region. The data were analysed using clustering for symbolic data and correspondence analysis. Distinct network types were identified based on the number and characteristics of the support providers, including relationship type, spatial proximity, and frequency of contact across emotional, instrumental and socialising dimensions. The identified types of social support networks are: family dependent, private restricted, local self-contained, locally integrated, and wider community network type, along with the cluster of egos that did not list any alter called “no-listed alters”. Trends regarding the prevalence of these network types suggest that while some were more strongly affected by the COVID-19 pandemic (no-listed alters, local self-contained, wider community), other network types (family dependent, private restricted, locally integrated) appear to reflect longer-term demographic changes. The partial recovery of informal social support is shown by the share of older adults with no social support having dropped to 4.4% by 2025. Nevertheless, persistent inequalities remain, with men in particular revealed to have greater chances of being socially isolated, pointing to the need for social services to provide proactive support targeting these groups.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the social support networks of older adults in Slovenia before, during, and after the COVID-19 pandemic. It examines the prevalence and characteristics of different types of social support networks, including informal support from relatives, friends, and neighbors, and how these networks have evolved over time, particularly in response to the pandemic.
Evidence base and method
The study is a cross-sectional survey conducted in Slovenia in 2002, 2020, and 2025, collecting ego-centred network data from older adults (65+). The data were analyzed using clustering for symbolic data and correspondence analysis. The samples are representative of both gender and region.
Consensus and conclusions
The review identifies five distinct types of social support networks: family dependent, private restricted, local self-contained, locally integrated, and wider community network. It also notes the emergence of a group called 'no-listed alters' who reported no informal social support. The prevalence of these network types suggests that some were more affected by the pandemic, while others reflect longer-term demographic changes. By 2025, the share of older adults with no social support had dropped to 4.4%, indicating partial recovery.
Controversies and gaps
Persistent inequalities in social support are observed, with men being more likely to experience social isolation. The review highlights the need for targeted social services to address these disparities. However, the evidence does not provide detailed mechanisms or explanations for these inequalities, nor does it explore the impact of specific SARS-CoV-2 variants on social support networks.
Research and practice implications
The findings suggest that social services should focus on proactive support for socially isolated older adults, particularly men. Further research is needed to understand the underlying reasons for persistent inequalities and to explore how different SARS-CoV-2 variants may influence social support dynamics.

Misinformation-based preventive behaviors during the COVID-19 pandemic in Myanmar: A cross-sectional study

Misinformation-based preventive behaviors during the COVID-19 pandemic in Myanmar: A cross-sectional study
作者： Win Min Thit, Seung Chun Paek, Mark Stephan Felix, Francois René Lamy
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本研究旨在探讨缅甸基于错误信息的预防行为 (MPB) 与社会人口特征、信息来源、社交媒体使用和健康信念因素之间的关联。
证据基础与方法
A cross-sectional online survey was conducted among adults residing in Myanmar, with data collected between March and May 2024. Data were collected using a structured questionnaire adapted from previous COVID-19 studies.
共识与结论
Use of social media platforms other than Facebook was associated with higher engagement in MPB (adjusted odds ratio [AOR] = 2.30, 95% confidence interval [CI]: 1.22–4.35). Participants reporting ≤3 hours and 4–6 hours of daily social media use had lower odds of MPB engagement compared with those reporting >6 hours (AOR = 0.46, 95% CI: 0.25–0.86; AOR = 0.48, 95% CI: 0.24–0.95, respectively). Higher self-efficacy was independently associated with greater engagement in MPB (AOR = 1.70, 95% CI: 1.03–2.82). MPB were more strongly associated with information environments and specific health beliefs than with sociodemographic characteristics alone.
争议与缺口
关于基于错误信息的预防行为的决定因素的证据仍然有限。
科研与实践启示
这些发现强调了解决错误信息和改善可靠健康信息的获取以减少参与非循证实践的重要性。

Misinformation-based preventive behaviors during the COVID-19 pandemic in Myanmar: A cross-sectional study

Authors: Win Min Thit, Seung Chun Paek, Mark Stephan Felix, Francois René Lamy
Original abstract (English)
Widespread misinformation influenced public understanding of disease risk and preventive behaviors during the COVID-19 pandemic, particularly in low-resource and conflict-affected settings such as Myanmar. Although preventive behaviors were generally protective, some actions driven by misinformation may have been ineffective or harmful. Evidence on determinants of misinformation-based preventive behaviors remained limited. This study aimed to examine associations between misinformation-based preventive behaviors (MPB) and sociodemographic characteristics, information sources, social media use, and health belief factors in Myanmar. A cross-sectional online survey was conducted among adults residing in Myanmar, with data collected between March and May 2024. Participants were asked to retrospectively report their preventive behaviors during the COVID-19 pandemic period (2020–2022). Data were collected using a structured questionnaire adapted from previous COVID-19 studies. The outcome variable was misinformation-based preventive behaviors, categorized as low (0–2 behaviors) and high (3–4 behaviors) engagement. Independent variables included sociodemographic characteristics, information sources, social media platform use, and selected health belief variables based on the Health Belief Model (HBM). A total of 434 respondents were included in the analysis, of whom 106 (24.4%) reported high engagement in MPB. Use of social media platforms other than Facebook was associated with higher engagement (adjusted odds ratio [AOR] = 2.30, 95% confidence interval [CI]: 1.22–4.35). Participants reporting ≤3 hours and 4–6 hours of daily social media use had lower odds of MPB engagement compared with those reporting >6 hours (AOR = 0.46, 95% CI: 0.25–0.86; AOR = 0.48, 95% CI: 0.24–0.95, respectively). Higher self-efficacy was independently associated with greater engagement in MPB (AOR = 1.70, 95% CI: 1.03–2.82). MPB were more strongly associated with information environments and specific health beliefs than with sociodemographic characteristics alone. These findings underscore the importance of addressing misinformation and improving access to reliable health information to reduce engagement in non–evidence-based practices.
View review five-element analysis

This study aimed to examine associations between misinformation-based preventive behaviors (MPB) and sociodemographic characteristics, information sources, social media use, and health belief factors in Myanmar.
Evidence base and method
A cross-sectional online survey was conducted among adults residing in Myanmar, with data collected between March and May 2024. Data were collected using a structured questionnaire adapted from previous COVID-19 studies.
Consensus and conclusions
Use of social media platforms other than Facebook was associated with higher engagement in MPB (adjusted odds ratio [AOR] = 2.30, 95% confidence interval [CI]: 1.22–4.35). Participants reporting ≤3 hours and 4–6 hours of daily social media use had lower odds of MPB engagement compared with those reporting >6 hours (AOR = 0.46, 95% CI: 0.25–0.86; AOR = 0.48, 95% CI: 0.24–0.95, respectively). Higher self-efficacy was independently associated with greater engagement in MPB (AOR = 1.70, 95% CI: 1.03–2.82). MPB were more strongly associated with information environments and specific health beliefs than with sociodemographic characteristics alone.
Controversies and gaps
Evidence on determinants of misinformation-based preventive behaviors remained limited.
Research and practice implications
These findings underscore the importance of addressing misinformation and improving access to reliable health information to reduce engagement in non–evidence-based practices.

Gender differences regarding body image-related aspects in the general population during the COVID-19 pandemic: a systematic review

Gender differences regarding body image-related aspects in the general population during the COVID-19 pandemic: a systematic review
作者： N. Laskowski, L. Sabel, Cristina Ballero Reque, M. Pahlenkemper, G. Brandt, C. Zaiser, G. Paslakis
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The review addresses how the COVID-19 pandemic (target disease) has affected body‐image related outcomes in the general adult population, focusing on gender differences in dissatisfaction, shame, and related constructs. Its central question is whether women and men differ in body‐image concerns during the pandemic.
证据基础与方法
The systematic review was preregistered on PROSPERO (CRD42023421727) and followed PRESS and PRISMA guidelines (A5). Searches were conducted in Scopus, Web of Science, PubMed, and PsycINFO for studies published between January 2019 and July 2024 (A6). Inclusion required original studies reporting gender‐specific analyses of body‐image outcomes in non‐clinical adults during COVID‐19 (A7). Sixteen studies with a total of 9,813 participants met the criteria (A10). Study quality was appraised with the Joanna Briggs Institute checklist (A8).
共识与结论
在大多数纳入的研究中,女性对身体的不满、体重和体型的担忧、身体羞耻、内疚和畸形的担忧程度高于男性 (A11)。男性通常表现出较低的不满情绪和较高的身体满意度,通常认为自己更苗条或更健壮(A12)。作者得出的结论是,尽管存在一些不一致之处,但在大流行期间,女性通常会经历更大的身体形象问题 (A16)。
争议与缺口
三项研究发现没有显着的性别差异,一项研究报告男性的满意度更高,表明研究结果存在异质性 (A13)。关于性少数和性别少数(SGM)个体的证据仍然很少且代表性不足(A15;A17)。此外,纵向数据有限,只有一项研究指出男性的不满情绪随着时间的推移而增加 (A14)。
科研与实践启示
作者呼吁开展进一步的纵向和性别多样化研究,以阐明公共卫生危机期间的身体意象轨迹,并指导有针对性的预防和干预策略(A18)。

Gender differences regarding body image-related aspects in the general population during the COVID-19 pandemic: a systematic review

Authors: N. Laskowski, L. Sabel, Cristina Ballero Reque, M. Pahlenkemper, G. Brandt, C. Zaiser, G. Paslakis
Original abstract (English)
Background The COVID-19 pandemic disrupted daily routines, physical activity, eating behaviors, and social interactions, all of which may have influenced body image. Previous research suggests that body image concerns differ by gender and may be particularly pronounced among sexual and gender minority (SGM) individuals, who face unique challenges due to internalized appearance ideals and societal stigma. This systematic review examined gender differences in body image-related concerns in the general adult population during the pandemic. Supported by: German Federal Ministry of Education and Research (01KX2121). Methods This systematic review was preregistered (PROSPERO CRD42023421727) and conducted according to PRESS, PRISMA guidelines. Scopus, Web of Science, PubMed, PsycINFO were searched for original studies published January 2019-July 2024. Studies were eligible if they 1) examined body image-related outcomes during the COVID-19 pandemic and reported gender-specific analyses in non-clinical adult populations. Study quality was assessed using the Joanna Briggs Institute Critical Appraisal Checklist for Analytical Cross-Sectional Studies. Studies were narratively summarized according to body image-related constructs assessed and the reported gender-specific findings. Results Sixteen studies comprising 9,813 participants were included. Across most studies, women reported greater body dissatisfaction, weight and shape concerns, body shame and guilt, and dysmorphic concerns than men. Men generally reported lower body dissatisfaction and higher body satisfaction and were more likely to perceive their bodies as slimmer or more athletic. However, three studies found no significant gender differences, and one study reported higher body dissatisfaction among men. Longitudinal evidence suggested increases in body dissatisfaction among men over the course of the pandemic, whereas weight concerns decreased among women over time. One study found higher levels of weight- and body-related shame and guilt among gender minority participants compared with cisgender men, while another study reported no association between sexual orientation and body appreciation. Conclusions Women generally reported greater body image-related concerns than men during the COVID-19 pandemic, although findings were not entirely consistent across studies. Evidence regarding SGM individuals remains limited due to their underrepresentation in the literature. Additional longitudinal and gender diverse research is needed to better understand body image changes during public health crises and to inform targeted prevention and intervention.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review addresses how the COVID-19 pandemic (target disease) has affected body‐image related outcomes in the general adult population, focusing on gender differences in dissatisfaction, shame, and related constructs. Its central question is whether women and men differ in body‐image concerns during the pandemic.
Evidence base and method
The systematic review was preregistered on PROSPERO (CRD42023421727) and followed PRESS and PRISMA guidelines (A5). Searches were conducted in Scopus, Web of Science, PubMed, and PsycINFO for studies published between January 2019 and July 2024 (A6). Inclusion required original studies reporting gender‐specific analyses of body‐image outcomes in non‐clinical adults during COVID‐19 (A7). Sixteen studies with a total of 9,813 participants met the criteria (A10). Study quality was appraised with the Joanna Briggs Institute checklist (A8).
Consensus and conclusions
Across the majority of included studies, women reported higher levels of body dissatisfaction, weight and shape concerns, body shame, guilt, and dysmorphic concerns than men (A11). Men generally showed lower dissatisfaction and higher body satisfaction, often perceiving themselves as slimmer or more athletic (A12). The authors conclude that women generally experienced greater body‐image concerns during the pandemic, despite some inconsistencies (A16).
Controversies and gaps
Three studies found no significant gender differences and one reported higher dissatisfaction among men, indicating heterogeneity in findings (A13). Evidence for sexual and gender minority (SGM) individuals remains sparse and under‐represented (A15; A17). Additionally, longitudinal data are limited, with only one study noting increasing dissatisfaction in men over time (A14).
Research and practice implications
The authors call for further longitudinal and gender‐diverse research to clarify body‐image trajectories during public‐health crises and to guide targeted prevention and intervention strategies (A18).

鼻内氮卓斯汀治疗 治疗 和预防 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2:随机试验的系统评价和探索性荟萃分析

Intranasal azelastine for the treatment and prevention of SARS-CoV-2: A systematic review and exploratory meta-analysis of randomized trials
作者： Shree Rath, Allahdad Khan, Mishaim Khan, Umama Alam, Waseef Ullah, Syed Ahtisham Halim
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述研究了鼻内氮卓斯汀治疗已确诊的 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 感染和预防意外感染的有效性和安全性,重点关注随机试验中的病毒学结果、预防效果和安全性。
证据基础与方法
截至 2026 年 1 月 16 日,系统检索了 PubMed/MEDLINE、EMBASE 和 Cochrane 图书馆,确定了比较鼻内氮卓斯汀与安慰剂的随机对照试验;使用 RoB-2 评估偏倚风险,使用 GRADE 评估确定性,并在适当的情况下采用随机效应荟萃分析。
共识与结论
中等质量的证据表明,在已确诊的感染中,鼻内氮卓斯汀在第 3 天可显着降低 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 病毒载量,但这一替代结果的临床相关性仍不确定。
争议与缺口
预防证据仅限于一项随机试验,治疗 和预防人群之间的安全性结果有所不同,强调了数据的探索性。
科研与实践启示
目前的证据不足以证实对患者重要结局的临床益处,强调需要进行更大规模、设计良好的试验来阐明疗效和安全性。

Intranasal azelastine for the treatment and prevention of SARS-CoV-2: A systematic review and exploratory meta-analysis of randomized trials

Authors: Shree Rath, Allahdad Khan, Mishaim Khan, Umama Alam, Waseef Ullah, Syed Ahtisham Halim
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND Azelastine, a widely used H1-antihistamine with demonstrated antiviral and antiinflammatory activity against severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) in preclinical and early clinical studies, has emerged as a potential repurposed therapy for early infection.This systematic review and meta-analysis aimed to evaluate the efficacy and safety of intranasal azelastine for the treatment of established SARS-CoV-2 infection and the prevention of incident SARS-CoV-2 infection.AIM To systematically evaluate the randomized evidence on intranasal azelastine for the treatment of established SARS-CoV-2 infection and the prevention of incident SARS-CoV-2 infection, with particular emphasis on virological outcomes, prevention efficacy, and safety.METHODS PubMed/MEDLINE, EMBASE, and Cochrane Library, together with trial registries and preprint servers, were searched through January 16, 2026.Randomized controlled trials evaluating intranasal azelastine vs placebo were identified through January 2026.Treatment efficacy was assessed using changes in SARS-CoV-2 viral load at predefined follow-up time points, while prevention efficacy was assessed using incident polymerase chain reaction (PCR)-confirmed SARS-CoV-2 infection.Safety outcomes were synthesized separately.Risk of bias was assessed using RoB-2 and certainty using Grading of Recommendations Assessment, Development and Evaluation.Randomeffects meta-analysis was used where quantitative pooling was appropriate. RESULTS / 45Three randomized controlled trials involving 754 participants were included.Two treatment trials contributed to the viral-load analyses, while one prevention trial evaluated incident SARS-CoV-2 infection.Among the two treatment trials, moderatecertainty evidence suggested a greater reduction in SARS-CoV-2 viral load at day 3 [mean difference (MD): -0.18, 95% confidence interval (CI): -0.25 to -0.12; I2 = 0%].The difference was smaller at day 5/6 (MD: -0.11, 95%CI: -0.18 to -0.05; I2 = 0%) and was not statistically significant at day 11 (MD: -0.09, 95%CI: -0.26 to 0.07; I2 = 4%).In the prevention trial, PCR-confirmed SARS-CoV-2 infection occurred in 5/227 participants (2.2%) receiving azelastine vs 15/223 (6.7%) receiving placebo [risk ratio (RR): 0.33, 95%CI: 0.12-0.89].Treatment-only all-adverse event pooling showed RR 0.85 (95%CI: 0.56-1.27;I2 = 61%), whereas the prevention trial showed RR 2.25 (95%CI: 1.64-3.09). CONCLUSIONModerate-certainty evidence suggested an early virological difference with intranasal azelastine in established SARS-CoV-2 infection, particularly at day 3, although the clinical relevance of this surrogate outcome remains uncertain.Evidence for prevention was based on a single randomized trial, while safety findings differed between treatment and prevention populations.Overall, the available evidence is exploratory and insufficient to establish clinical benefit on patient-important outcomes.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examined the efficacy and safety of intranasal azelastine for treating established SARS‐CoV‐2 infection and preventing incident infection, focusing on virological outcomes, prevention efficacy, and safety in randomized trials.
Evidence base and method
A systematic search of PubMed/MEDLINE, EMBASE, and Cochrane Library through January 16, 2026 identified randomized controlled trials comparing intranasal azelastine to placebo; risk of bias was assessed with RoB‐2 and certainty with GRADE, and random‐effects meta‐analysis was employed where appropriate.
Consensus and conclusions
Moderate‐certainty evidence indicated a greater reduction in SARS‐CoV‐2 viral load at day 3 with intranasal azelastine in established infection, though the clinical relevance of this surrogate outcome remains uncertain.
Controversies and gaps
Evidence for prevention was limited to a single randomized trial, and safety findings varied between treatment and prevention populations, underscoring the exploratory nature of the data.
Research and practice implications
The current evidence is insufficient to confirm clinical benefit on patient‐important outcomes, highlighting the need for larger, well‐designed trials to clarify efficacy and safety.

First-hand Alcohol-Related Harms in Hong Kong University Dormitories: A Serial Cross-sectional Study Comparing the Normative, Social Unrest, and COVID-19 Pandemic Periods.

First-hand Alcohol-Related Harms in Hong Kong University Dormitories: A Serial Cross-sectional Study Comparing the Normative, Social Unrest, and COVID-19 Pandemic Periods.
作者： M. Liu, J. Lee, Elorm Donkor, Lai-Kit So, Jean H. Kim
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
The study examines first-hand alcohol-related harms among university dormitory residents in Hong Kong during three distinct periods: the normative period, the time of social unrest (protests), and the COVID-19 pandemic. The central question is whether alcohol-related behaviors and their associated harms differ across these periods.
证据基础与方法
该研究是2018年9月至2021年6月期间进行的系列横断面调查,涉及2659名参与者。它比较了三个时期的饮酒行为和第一手伤害:正常时期、社会动荡时期和大流行时期。该方法包括使用调整后的比值比对关联进行统计分析。
共识与结论
研究发现,饮酒水平在正常时期和抗议时期保持稳定,但在大流行期间有所增加。在正常时期、抗议时期和大流行时期,报告与酒精有关的伤害的参与者比例分别为 57.1%、53.9% 和 62.5%。在这三个时期,酗酒始终与较高的第一手伤害相关。
争议与缺口
该研究没有提供有关饮酒与第一手伤害之间联系的机制或干预措施有效性的详细信息。它还没有探讨文化或政策因素在这些时期塑造饮酒行为的作用。
科研与实践启示
研究结果表明,东亚大学宿舍需要针对具体情况制定减少酒精危害的策略,特别是在大流行等社会和健康危机时期。

First-hand Alcohol-Related Harms in Hong Kong University Dormitories: A Serial Cross-sectional Study Comparing the Normative, Social Unrest, and COVID-19 Pandemic Periods.

Authors: M. Liu, J. Lee, Elorm Donkor, Lai-Kit So, Jean H. Kim
Original abstract (English)
Between September 2018 and June 2021, we conducted serial cross-sectional surveys of university dormitory residents in Hong Kong (n = 2659). Drinking behaviors and first-hand harms were examined across a normative period, time of protests, and the COVID-19 pandemic. Drinking levels remained steady between the normative and protest periods but increased during the pandemic, and the proportions reporting drinking-related harms were 57.1%, 53.9%, and 62.5%, respectively. Binge drinking was associated with high first-hand harms across 3 periods (adjusted odds ratio [AOR]: 3.46-5.76). Having a drinking roommate was also correlated with harms during the normative period (AOR = 1.66). During the protests, underage drinking history and university location were associated with high first-hand harms (AOR: 1.69 and 1.82). During the COVID-19 pandemic, weekend dormitory residence and low alcohol-related knowledge were significant factors (AOR: 2.78 and 2.85). The findings indicate the need for context-specific alcohol harm reduction strategies in East Asian university dormitories.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The study examines first-hand alcohol-related harms among university dormitory residents in Hong Kong during three distinct periods: the normative period, the time of social unrest (protests), and the COVID-19 pandemic. The central question is whether alcohol-related behaviors and their associated harms differ across these periods.
Evidence base and method
The study is a serial cross-sectional survey conducted between September 2018 and June 2021, involving 2659 participants. It compares drinking behaviors and first-hand harms across three time periods: normative, social unrest, and the pandemic. The methodology includes statistical analysis of associations using adjusted odds ratios.
Consensus and conclusions
The study found that drinking levels remained stable between the normative and protest periods but increased during the pandemic. The proportions of participants reporting alcohol-related harms were 57.1%, 53.9%, and 62.5% for the normative, protest, and pandemic periods, respectively. Binge drinking was consistently associated with higher first-hand harms across all three periods.
Controversies and gaps
The study does not provide detailed information on the mechanisms linking alcohol consumption to first-hand harms or on the effectiveness of interventions. It also does not explore the role of cultural or policy factors in shaping drinking behaviors during these periods.
Research and practice implications
The findings suggest the need for context-specific alcohol harm reduction strategies in East Asian university dormitories, particularly during periods of social and health crises like the pandemic.

🚨 突发动态与新闻 / Health News

Published: 2026-11-19  |  Publisher: GOV.UK
A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

国家流感和 新型冠状病毒感染 监测 报告:2026 年 11 月 19 日(第 47 周) - GOV.UK

National flu and COVID-19 surveillance report: 19 November 2026 (week 47) - GOV.UK
新闻简报2026 年 11 月 19 日(第 47 周)的国家流感和 新型冠状病毒感染 监测 报告概述了英格兰的健康状况。报告指出,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 活动保持稳定,第 46 周期间医院环境中有 1,826 例确诊病例。流感活动增加,滚动 7 天阳性率为 11.4%,而前一周为 8.3%。甲型流感阳性率升至10.1%,乙型流感阳性率稳定在0.3%。RSV 活动也增加,急性细支气管炎急诊就诊人数增加。该报告强调,流感的传播程度高于基线水平,并且已向国民医疗服务体系(NHS)发出了一份简报。该数据基于监测系统,并会进行回顾性更新。用于确定活动水平的方法,例如移动流行病方法 (MEM) 和平均标准差 (MSD),是实验性的,可能会进行修订。检测政策随着时间的推移而发生变化,这可能会影响阳性率,因此应谨慎进行比较。该报告于2026年11月19日发布,数据截至2026年11月15日。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该报告于2026年11月19日发布,引用的数据截至2026年第46周(2026年11月9日至11月15日)。
地点与对象
该报告重点关注英格兰,特别提到了 75 岁及以上人口、75 岁以下免疫系统较弱的人口以及 85 岁及以上等年龄组。
事件
The report details the surveillance findings for SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19), influenza, and RSV in England during week 46 of 2026. It notes that SARS-CoV-2 activity remained stable, influenza activity increased, and RSV activity also increased.
规模、影响与风险
截至 2026 年 11 月 17 日,第 46 周,医院内确诊了 1,826 例 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 病例。流感阳性率从前一周的 8.3% 上升至 11.4%,其中甲型流感阳性率为 10.1%,乙型流感阳性率为 0.3%。RSV 活动增加,急性细支气管炎急诊就诊人数增加。
应对、状态与不确定性
报告指出,流感活动高于基线水平,并向 NHS 发出了简报。该数据基于监测系统,并会进行回顾性更新。所使用的方法(MEM 和 MSD)是实验性的,可能会进行修订,并且由于测试政策的潜在变化,应谨慎解释随时间的比较。
A high priorityPublic-health news

National flu and COVID-19 surveillance report: 19 November 2026 (week 47) - GOV.UK

News Brief
The National Flu and COVID-19 Surveillance Report for 19 November 2026 (week 47) provides an overview of the health situation in England. It notes that SARS-CoV-2 activity remained stable, with 1,826 confirmed cases in hospital settings during week 46. Influenza activity increased, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 11.4% compared to 8.3% in the previous week. Influenza A positivity rose to 10.1%, while influenza B remained stable at 0.3%. RSV activity also increased, with ED attendances for acute bronchiolitis rising. The report highlights that influenza is circulating above baseline levels and that a briefing note has been issued to the NHS. The data is based on surveillance systems and is subject to retrospective updates. The methods used to determine activity levels, such as the Moving Epidemic Method (MEM) and Mean Standard Deviation (MSD), are experimental and may be revised. Testing policies have changed over time, which could affect positivity rates, so comparisons should be made cautiously. The report was published on 19 November 2026, with data up to 15 November 2026.
Original source text (English)
This publication is licensed under the terms of the Open Government Licence v3.0 except where otherwise stated. To view this licence, visit nationalarchives.gov.uk/doc/open-government-licence/version/3 or write to the Information Policy Team, The National Archives, Kew, London TW9 4DU, or email: psi@nationalarchives.gov.uk . Where we have identified any third party copyright information you will need to obtain permission from the copyright holders concerned. This publication is available at https://www.gov.uk/government/statistics/national-flu-and-covid-19-surveillance-reports-2026-to-2027-season/national-flu-and-covid-19-surveillance-report-19-november-2026-week-47 This report summarises the information from the surveillance systems which are used to monitor COVID-19 (caused by severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2)), influenza, respiratory syncytial virus ( RSV ) and diseases caused by other seasonal respiratory viruses in England. The report is based on data up to week 46 of 2026 (9 November to 15 November 2026). The main messages of this report are: Annual reports for the 2025 to 2026 season: Influenza in the UK, annual epidemiological report: winter 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Epidemiology of COVID-19 in England (published 25 June 2026) Seasonal influenza vaccine uptake in all GP patients: annual report 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Seasonal influenza vaccine uptake in school-aged children: annual report 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Seasonal influenza vaccine uptake in frontline healthcare workers: annual report 2025 to 2026 (published 28 May 2026) Influenza activity increased and is circulating at low levels. Emergency department ( ED ) attendances for influenza-like-illness ( ILI ) increased and were above seasonally expected levels. The number of influenza-confirmed acute respiratory infection ( ARI ) incidents increased compared with the previous week. A briefing note has been issued to inform the NHS that influenza activity is now above baseline levels. Compared with the equivalent week last season (week 46 data, 2025 to 2026), vaccine uptake is lower among patients aged 65 years and over (65.8% vs 67.8%), patients aged 6 months to under 65 years in clinical risk groups (32.5% vs 33.5%), those aged 2 years (37.6% vs 38.5%), and those aged 3 years (37.5% vs 38.8%). In contrast, vaccine uptake is higher among pregnant women (33.9% vs 32.8%). COVID-19 activity was stable and is circulating at low levels. ED attendances for COVID-19-like illness decreased. The number of COVID-19 confirmed acute respiratory infections ( ARI ) incidents increased compared with the previous week. By the end of week 46 2026, 54.5% of all people aged 75 years and over in England had been vaccinated with an autumn 2026 dose. Similarly, 23.1% of all people aged under 75 years with a weakened immune system in England had been vaccinated with an autumn 2026 dose since 1 October 2026. RSV activity increased and is circulating at low levels. ED attendances for acute bronchiolitis increased and were below seasonally expected levels. Note 1: these indicators use the moving epidemic method ( MEM ) and the mean standard deviation method ( MSD ) to define thresholds to determine their respective levels of activity. Further information on these methods can be found in Influenza surveillance in Europe: establishing epidemic thresholds by the Moving Epidemic Method and Setting thresholds to determine COVID-19 activity levels using the mean standard deviation ( MSD ) method, England, 2022 to 2024 . The MEM approach is well-established for some influenza surveillance indicators, however, for other indicators both the MEM and MSD are experimental and may be subject to future revision. Influenza laboratory surveillance and GP swabbing positivity have transitioned from using MEM to using MSD . These approaches will be considered alongside expert opinion and triangulation of other data sources. The Second Generation Surveillance System ( SGSS ) captures test result information for notifiable infectious diseases, including COVID-19 and influenza, from laboratories in England. The unified sample dataset ( USD ) stores all SARS-CoV-2 test results reported to SGSS , Respiratory DataMart and UKHSA laboratories, and is used to calculate the percentage of tests positive for SARS-CoV-2 among all SARS-CoV-2 tests conducted. As of 17 November 2026, there were a total of 1,826 COVID-19 cases identified in hospital settings in week 46, remaining stable compared with 1,792 cases in the previous week. SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19) PCR positivity in hospital settings remained stable in week 46, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 7.0% up to Sunday 15 November 2026 compared with 7.2% for the same period on the previous Sunday. Positivity rates were highest among those aged 85 years and over with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 9.9% up to Sunday 15 November 2026. This has remained stable compared with 10.4% in the same age group on the previous Sunday. Note 2: data from previous seasons is aligned by day. Note 3: testing policy and practice may change over time which can impact positivity rates, therefore comparisons over time should be interpreted with caution. Notable changes in testing policy occurred during 2022 to 2023, which are outlined in the data quality report . Note 4: the highlighted line corresponds to the age group in the subplot title, grey lines correspond to all other age groups. Influenza positivity increased in week 46, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 11.4% up to Sunday 15 November 2026. This is compared with 8.3% on the previous Sunday. By type, influenza A positivity increased in week 46, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 10.1% up to Sunday 15 November 2026. Influenza B positivity remained stable in the same week, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 0.3% up to Sunday 15 November 2026. Influenza A positivity rates were highest among those aged between 5 and 11 years, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 23.2% up to Sunday 15 November 2026. This has increased from 13.3% in the same age group on the previous Sunday. Influenza B positivity rates for the same period were highest among those aged between 12 and 17 years, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 2.1%. This has increased from 1.4% in the same age group on the previous Sunday. RSV swab-positivity-based PCR test results reported through SGSS have been included as a pilot indicator from week 49 2025 onwards. RSV positivity increased in week 46, with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 5.1% up to Sunday 15 November 2026, compared with 3.2% up to the previous Sunday. RSV positivity rates were highest in those aged between 0 and 4 years with a rolling 7-day positivity rate of 28.8% up to Sunday 15 November 2026 in week 46. This has increased from 19.1% in the same age group on the previous Sunday. Respiratory DataMart is a sentinel laboratory-based surveillance system where participating laboratories report positive and negative test results for a number of respiratory viruses from samples primarily taken in hospital. A small proportion of primary care samples are also included in this reporting. In week 46, data is based on reporting from 9 out of the 14 sentinel laboratories. In week 46, 5,156 respiratory specimens reported through the Respiratory DataMart System were tested for influenza. There were 588 positive samples for influenza: 397 influenza A (not subtyped), 7 influenza A (H3N2), 166 influenza A (H1N1)pdm09, and 20 influenza B. Overall, influenza positivity increased to 11.4% in week 46 compared with 8.0% in the previous week. In week 46, 5,437 respiratory specimens reported through the Respiratory DataMart System were tested for SARS-CoV-2. There were 346 positive samples for SARS-CoV-2. SARS-CoV-2 positivity increased slightly to 6.4% compared with 6.0% in the previous week, with the highest positivity in those aged 80 years and over at 9.3%. RSV positivity increased to 5.2%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 26.7%. Adenovirus positivity decreased slightly to 1.6%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 5.1%. Human metapneumovirus ( hMPV ) positivity increased to 0.7%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 1.7%. Parainfluenza positivity decreased slightly to 2.9%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 8.7%. Rhinovirus positivity decreased to 11.3%, with the highest positivity in those aged under 5 years at 28.5%. DataMart data is provisional and subject to retrospective updates. Note 5: shading represents 95% confidence intervals. UKHSA conducts genomic surveillance of SARS-CoV-2 lineages. This section provides an overview of circulating lineages in England, derived from data on sequenced PCR -positive SARS-CoV-2 samples in SGSS . The prevalence of UKHSA -designated lineages among sequenced cases is presented in Figure 13. To account for reporting delays, we report the proportion of lineages within COVID-19 cases that have had a sequenced positive sample between 12 October 2026 and 25 October 2026. Note that low sequencing numbers, especially within the latest reporting period, will impact the accuracy of the prevalence estimates. These most recent figures should therefore be interpreted with caution. Note that lineages will be grouped independently from their parent lineage once they reach sufficient prevalence, and may be re-grouped into their parent lineage if their prevalence subsequently falls. The data quality report contains more information on lineage groupings. Analysis of viruses from primary and secondary care shows that circulating A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses show low genetic diversity and all belong to clade D.3.1.1. Observations published from the World Health Organization (WHO) influenza vaccine composition meeting (VCM) in September 2026 suggest low reactivity of some viruses within the D.3.1.1 subclade with post-infection ferret antisera raised against A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1)pdm09-like virus – the A(H1N1)pdm09 component in 2026 Southern and 2026/2027 Northern Hemisphere cell- and egg-based influenza vaccines - and human sera post-vaccination with the Southern Hemisphere vaccine 2026. It is not yet clear how vaccine effectiveness against clinical disease may be affected by these observations. Vaccines typically still provide protection against severe clinical disease when drifted strains are seen with only a modest reduction in effectiveness. The characterisation of circulating A(H3N2) viruses shows that A(H3N2) viruses are currently showing low genetic diversity. All sequenced viruses to date belong to genetic subclade K. To date, only a small number of influenza B viruses have been detected, which show some genetic diversity. Between week 35 2026 (week ending 30 August 2026) and week 46 2026 (week ending 15 November 2026), the UKHSA respiratory virus unit ( RVU ) has genetically characterised 477 seasonal influenza viruses, and identified 343 influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses, 100 influenza A(H3N2) viruses, and 34 influenza B viruses. Details of the characterised viruses by subtype are shown in Tables 1, 2 and 3. The RVU has confirmed by genome sequencing the detection of live attenuated influenza vaccine ( LAIV ) viruses in 17 influenza A/B positive samples, collected from children aged between 2 and 16 years. UKHSA RVU performs antigenic characterisation of influenza A(H1N1)pdm09, influenza A(H3N2) and influenza B viruses using haemagglutination inhibition ( HI ) assays. Data from these assays are used to compare how similar the currently circulating influenza viruses are to the strains included in seasonal influenza vaccines, and to monitor for changes in circulating influenza viruses. Similarity of currently circulating influenza strains to vaccine strains is defined as having an antibody titre within 4-fold when compared with reference viruses representative of the vaccine strain. Antigenic characterisation of viruses in the current reporting period is underway. A(H1N1)pdm09: 13 A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses have been antigenically characterised and 11 were similar to reference viruses representative of the A/Missouri/11/2025 (H1N1)pdm09-like Northern Hemisphere 2026/27 (H1N1)pdm09 vaccine strains. A(H3N2): 6 A(H3N2) viruses have been antigenically characterised and 6 were similar to reference viruses representative of the A/Darwin/1454/2025 (H3N2)-like and A/Darwin/1415/2025 (H3N2)-like Northern Hemisphere 2026/27 (H3N2) vaccine strains. Influenza positive samples are screened for mutations in the virus neuraminidase ( NA ) and the cap-dependent endonuclease of the polymerase acidic protein (PA) genes known to confer neuraminidase inhibitor (oseltamivir and zanamivir) or baloxavir resistance, respectively. Results from this surveillance are given in Table 4. There have been 9 detections with mutations related to reduced susceptibility/resistance to oseltamivir and/or reduced susceptibility to baloxavir. Data is presented on viral ARI incidents in different settings that are reported to UKHSA health protection teams ( HPTs ). Please note that reporting practices are known to vary between seasons and between regions. Any interpretation of temporal and regional trends should consider the likelihood of differences in reporting of ARI incidents over time and between regions. There were 147 new ARI incidents reported in week 46 in England. These included: 119 incidents from care homes, of which 26 were due to influenza A, 17 were due to SARS-CoV-2, 17 were due to influenza (no type information available), 7 were due to multiple pathogens, 5 were due to other pathogens and 3 were due to RSV . No pathogen was reported in 44 incidents 1 incident from hospitals, of which 1 was due to SARS-CoV-2 21 incidents from educational settings, of which 6 were due to influenza A, 5 were due to RSV and 1 was due to influenza (no type information available). No pathogen was reported in 9 incidents 1 incident from prisons, of which 1 was due to influenza A 5 incidents from other settings, of which 3 were due to SARS-CoV-2 and 1 was due to influenza (no type information available). No pathogen was reported in 1 incident FluSurvey is an internet-based participatory surveillance system based on the InfluenzaNet platform. It monitors trends of influenza-like illness ( ILI ) in the community using self-reported respiratory symptoms from registered participants. As a participatory surveillance system, new signups can occur throughout the season and participation can vary in each week. The European Centre for Disease Prevention and Control ( ECDC ) ILI case definition of sudden onset of symptoms with at least one of fever (chills), malaise, headache, muscle pain and at least one of cough, sore throat, shortness of breath is used for reporting. Please note that ILI is a broad definition and can include other respiratory illnesses such as COVID-19. Healthcare use is presented as self-reported use of health services among participants meeting the ILI ECDC case definition. Where a person reports use of more than one health care service, secondary care will be indicated over primary care use and physical attendance to primary care will be indicated over use of remote services (for example, online NHS services, telephoning their GP or 111). During week commencing 9 November 2026: there were 2,522 participants who completed the weekly symptoms questionnaire 396 (15.7%) reported fever or cough and 129 (5.1%) met the ILI case definition, in the past week 15.5% of participants meeting the ILI case definition reported contact with healthcare services as a result of self-reported symptoms; the most frequently reported contact was a visit to the GP the proportion of participants meeting the ILI case definition remained stable compared with the previous report week (5.1% compared with 5.1% in week 45) data submitted in the current week (commencing 16 November) is available on the UKHSA data dashboard to support more timely insights. For further details see the data quality report Syndromic surveillance collects data from various healthcare sources where presentations are classified by patterns of symptoms compatible with specific infections. In some settings, the syndromic diagnosis can be supplemented by (rapid) testing. In this report, ED attendances are displayed. Further details and data from other syndromic surveillance systems can be found in the syndromic surveillance weekly summaries . During the week ending on 15 November 2026, ED attendances for acute respiratory infections increased and were similar to the seasonally expected level. ED attendances for COVID-19-like illness increased. ED attendances for influenza-like illness increased and were above seasonally expected levels. ED attendances for acute bronchiolitis (a syndrome related to RSV infection) increased and were below seasonally expected levels. NHS 111 triaged calls and online assessments for acute respiratory infections increased in children under the age of 15 years, and remained at seasonally expected levels. GP in-hours consultation rates for upper and lower respiratory tract infections increased, notably in children aged 1 to 4 and 5 to 14 years but remained at seasonally expected levels. Consultation rates for influenza-like illness also increased and are above expected levels. GP out-of-hours daily contacts for respiratory conditions increased, including acute respiratory infections, influenza-like illness and acute bronchitis/bronchiolitis. Contacts for acute respiratory infections and acute bronchitis/bronchiolitis remained at or below baseline levels, while influenza-like illness was above expected levels. View five news elements
Time
The report was published on 19 November 2026, and the data it references covers up to week 46 of 2026 (9 November to 15 November 2026).
Location and population
The report focuses on England, with specific mention of populations aged 75 years and over, those under 75 with weakened immune systems, and age groups such as 85 years and over.
Event
The report details the surveillance findings for SARS-CoV-2 (COVID-19), influenza, and RSV in England during week 46 of 2026. It notes that SARS-CoV-2 activity remained stable, influenza activity increased, and RSV activity also increased.
Scale, impact and risk
As of 17 November 2026, there were 1,826 confirmed SARS-CoV-2 cases in hospital settings during week 46. Influenza positivity increased to 11.4% compared to 8.3% in the previous week, with influenza A positivity at 10.1% and influenza B at 0.3%. RSV activity increased, with acute bronchiolitis ED attendances rising.
Response, status and uncertainty
The report indicates that influenza activity is above baseline levels, and a briefing note has been issued to the NHS. The data is based on surveillance systems and is subject to retrospective updates. The methods used (MEM and MSD) are experimental and may be revised, and comparisons over time should be interpreted with caution due to potential changes in testing policy.
